<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the sixth-symmetric-power case of Foulkes' conjecture. For every integer <i>b</i> ≥ 6 and every finite-dimensional complex vector space <i>V</i>, there is a $`\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}\nolimits (V)`$-equivariant injection $`\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}\nolimits ^6(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}\nolimits ^b V)\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}\nolimits ^b(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}\nolimits ^6 V)`$.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $b\ge6$ and every finite-dimensional complex vector space $V$, there is a $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(V)$-equivariant injection $$\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^6(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^b V)\ \mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21AA}% \OriginalHookrightarrow\EndAccSupp{}}\ \mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^b(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^6 V).$$ Equivalently, $h_b[h_6]-h_6[h_b]$ is Schur-positive for every $b\ge6$.
  - Explanation: The sixth symmetric-power comparison holds in every finite complex dimension.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-quadratic-range)): For every finite-dimensional complex $V$ and every $b\ge30$, there is a $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(V)$-equivariant injection $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^6(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^b V)\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21AA}% \OriginalHookrightarrow\EndAccSupp{}}\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^b(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^6 V)$.
  - Explanation: Quadratic stabilization settles the large-degree range by an equivariant section.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rectangles)): Let $c,d\in\mathbb Z_{\ge 0}^6$ satisfy $6\mid w(c)$ and $6\mid w(d)$. If $Q(c)>0$, then $Q(d+c)\ge Q(d)$. In particular $$Q(d+6e_i)\ge Q(d)\quad(1\le i\le6), \qquad F(d+6e_6)=F(d).$$
  - Explanation: Highest-weight multiplication transports target lower bounds; determinant shifts preserve source multiplicities.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-chart)): For every integer $t\ge0$, every row-permutation-invariant polynomial in the lists $Y_i$ of total degree at most $t$ is a linear combination of products of at most $t$ elements of $z^{-1}R_1$. Consequently, for integers $k,L\ge0$ and $f\in R_k$, $$z^Lf\in A_{k+L}\qquad\text{if }k+L\ge ak.$$
  - Explanation: Multisymmetric factor length bounds clear denominators in the multiplication image.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-first-row)): For every integer $b\ge1$ and partition $\lambda$ of $6b$ with $\sum_{i\ge2}\lambda_i\le b$, one has $$[s_\lambda]h_6[h_b]\le [s_\lambda]h_b[h_6].$$
  - Explanation: The chart settles partitions whose tail sum is at most the degree.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-polarization)): For integers $L\ge1$ and $j\ge aL$, $$W_j=\sum_{v\in V}z(v)^L W_{j-L}, \qquad R_j=\sum_{v\in V}z(v)^L R_{j-L}.$$ The sums mean linear spans.
  - Explanation: Polarization expresses the graded spaces as spans of pure-power multiples.
- **C7** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-module-generation)): The $A$-module $R$ is generated by $R_0,\ldots,R_{a-1}$.
  - Explanation: Low-degree components generate the invariant ring as a module.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-large)): For $a\ge2$, one has $R_b=A_b$ whenever $b\ge a(a-1)^2$.
  - Explanation: Chart denominator clearing and polarization give an independent large-degree range.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-strips)): Let $\gamma,\mu$ be partitions of length at most $n$, and suppose $\|\gamma\|-\|\mu\|=ib$, where $i,b\ge1$. Then $$[s_\gamma]\,h_b(X^i)s_\mu(X)\in\{0,1,-1\}.$$ For a nonzero coefficient one necessarily has $$\gamma_j\ge\mu_j,\qquad \mu_j\ge\gamma_{j+i} \quad\text{whenever the subscripts are in range}.$$ Here $X^i=(x_1^i,\ldots,x_n^i)$.
  - Explanation: Signed strip coefficients and containment inequalities govern exact removal steps.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-upper)): The function $U$ is nondecreasing in every gap. For every nonnegative gap vector $d$ with $6\mid w(d)$, one has $F(d)\le U(d)$.
  - Explanation: Counting intermediate coordinates bounds source multiplicities monotonically in the gaps.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sumset)): Let $D$ be a nonempty finite set of $C$ points in a real vector space, of affine dimension $h$. Write $kD=\{v_1+\cdots+v_k:v_1,\ldots,v_k\in D\}$. For this $k$-fold sumset, $k\ge1$, one has $$\|kD\|\ \ge\ f(C,h,k):=\binom{k+h}{h}+(C-1-h)\binom{k+h-1}{h}.$$
  - Explanation: A triangulation gives a lower bound for repeated finite sumsets.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-power)): Suppose $Q(x)\ge C>0$, where $C$ is an integer and $w(x)=6d_0$ with $d_0>0$. Put $$z=\min\left(18,\ \min\left\{h\ge0: \binom{d_0+h}{h}\ge C\right\}\right).$$ Then for all $k\ge1$, $$\label{eq:power} Q(kx)\ge f(C,z,k).$$
  - Explanation: Distinct leading monomials amplify target multiplicity lower bounds.
- **C13** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-ver-certificates)): The complete program in Appendix <a href="#app:certificate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:certificate">8</a> verifies the base comparisons in Table <a href="#tab:base" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tab:base">1</a>. Its mode list has $87354$ points, or $114314$ after taking the downward closure. It flags $4969698$ pairs in the middle interval, all with $t_2(d)\le27$. Their counts by degree range appear in Table <a href="#tab:band" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tab:band">2</a>.
  - Explanation: Finite comparisons and propagated lower bounds reduce the remaining cases to a tail band.
- **C14** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-ver-band)): The complete program in Appendix <a href="#app:band" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:band">9</a> checks $57065668$ coefficients in this band. Of these, $177134$ are zero and none are negative; every integer in <a href="#eq:finalinteger" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finalinteger">[eq:finalinteger]</a> is divisible by $720$. The grouped counts appear in Table <a href="#tab:band" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tab:band">2</a>.
  - Explanation: Exact band coefficients supply the residual nonnegativity comparisons.

          "source_id": "PM-210.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-thm-main"
    },

        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Schur module and character** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Write $\mathbf S_\lambda V$ for the Schur module indexed by a partition $\lambda$, and $s_\lambda$ for its character.
- **plethysm character** ([source](#pm-h-2)): If $h_r$ denotes the complete homogeneous symmetric function, then $h_a[h_b]$ is the stable character of $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^a(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^b V)$, where brackets denote plethysm.
- **graded invariant ring** ([source](#eq:multmap)): For the moment let $a\ge2$, and set $$P=(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}V)^{\otimes a},\qquad W_j=P_{(j,\ldots,j)},\qquad R_j=W_j^{\mathfrak S_a},\qquad R=\bigoplus_{j\ge0}R_j.$$
- **multiplication subalgebra** ([source](#eq:multmap)): Let $A\subset R$ be the graded subalgebra generated by $R_1=\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^a V$.
- **pure-power element** ([source](#eq:multmap)): For $v\in V$, put $z(v)=v^{\otimes a}\in R_1$.
- **gap coordinates** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Pad every partition by zeros to six parts and write $$d_i=\lambda_i-\lambda_{i+1},\quad \lambda_7=0,\qquad \lambda(d)_i=\sum_{j=i}^6d_j,\qquad w(d)=\sum_{i=1}^6 i\,d_i.$$
- **tail sum** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Also put $t_s(d)=\sum_{j>s}\lambda(d)_j$.
- **signed lookup** ([source](#pm-h-13)): For an integer vector $v$ of length $n$, a *signed lookup* is zero if $v$ has a negative entry or a repeated entry. Otherwise sort $v$ decreasingly, subtract $\delta_n$, and multiply the coefficient at that partition by the sorting sign.
- **source recurrence coefficients** ([source](#eq:sourcerec)): For the source, fix $b$ and set $$H_j(\gamma)=[s_\gamma]h_j[h_b],\qquad H_0(0)=1.$$
- **upper-certificate widths** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For a nonnegative gap vector $d$, define $$h_{sj}=1+\lambda(d)_j-\lambda(d)_{j+6-s},\qquad N_s(d)=\frac{\prod_{j=1}^s h_{sj}}{\max_{1\le j\le s}h_{sj}} \quad(2\le s\le5),\qquad N_0=N_1=1.$$
- **propagation** ([source](#pm-h-17)): For an array on a downward-closed set, *propagation* replaces its value at $m$ by the maximum of all values at indices $u\le m$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R2** [claim](#stmt-cor-quadratic-range) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-quadratic-range): Quadratic stabilization settles the large-degree range by an equivariant section. -> Quadratic stabilization settles the large-degree range by an equivariant section.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-rectangles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rectangles): Highest-weight multiplication transports target lower bounds; determinant shifts preserve source multiplicities. -> Highest-weight multiplication transports target lower bounds; determinant shifts preserve source multiplicities.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-chart) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chart): Multisymmetric factor length bounds clear denominators in the multiplication image. -> Multisymmetric factor length bounds clear denominators in the multiplication image.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-cor-first-row) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-first-row): The chart settles partitions whose tail sum is at most the degree. -> The chart settles partitions whose tail sum is at most the degree.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-polarization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-polarization): Polarization expresses the graded spaces as spans of pure-power multiples. -> Polarization expresses the graded spaces as spans of pure-power multiples.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-cor-module-generation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-module-generation): Low-degree components generate the invariant ring as a module. -> Low-degree components generate the invariant ring as a module.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-lem-strips) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-strips): Signed strip coefficients and containment inequalities govern exact removal steps. -> Signed strip coefficients and containment inequalities govern exact removal steps.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-prop-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-upper): Counting intermediate coordinates bounds source multiplicities monotonically in the gaps. -> Counting intermediate coordinates bounds source multiplicities monotonically in the gaps.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-lem-sumset) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sumset): A triangulation gives a lower bound for repeated finite sumsets. -> A triangulation gives a lower bound for repeated finite sumsets.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-prop-power) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-power): Distinct leading monomials amplify target multiplicity lower bounds. -> Distinct leading monomials amplify target multiplicity lower bounds.
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-ver-certificates) / [proof](#sec:certificates): Finite comparisons and propagated lower bounds reduce the remaining cases to a tail band. -> Finite comparisons and propagated lower bounds reduce the remaining cases to a tail band.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-ver-band) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ver-band): Exact band coefficients supply the residual nonnegativity comparisons. -> Exact band coefficients supply the residual nonnegativity comparisons.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-large) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-large): Chart denominator clearing and polarization give an independent large-degree range. -> Chart denominator clearing and polarization give an independent large-degree range.
- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ver-band): The sixth symmetric-power comparison holds in every finite complex dimension. -> The sixth symmetric-power comparison holds in every finite complex dimension.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **strip-coefficient**: The signed strip coefficient is zero, positive one, or negative one; a nonzero coefficient requires both stated containment inequalities.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **full-proof**: Treat agreement of a signed-strip subroutine as certification of the full equivariant injection. -> The complete comparison additionally needs target multiplicities, upper and lower certificates, range coverage, and the large-degree argument.
- **unsigned**: Discard signs from exact strip coefficients. -> Exact source recurrences require cancellation; an unsigned count only supports an upper bound.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The signed strip rule gives a finite exact coefficient calculation from partitions and a positive removal size. This implements that calculation, rather than the full multiplicity comparison.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of records gamma, mu, i, b.
# PRECONDITIONS: Padded nonnegative decreasing partitions of equal length; i,b positive; size difference i*b.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of exact signed-strip coefficients in input order.
# TIME: Polynomial determinant work per record; input size controls the finite batch.
# SPACE: Quadratic matrix storage per record and linear output storage.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact arbitrary-precision integers with python-flint fmpz_mat.
# FAILURE: Invalid records raise ValueError; no approximate result is returned.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz_mat
answers = []
for record in json.load(sys.stdin):
    g, m, i, b = (record[k] for k in ('gamma', 'mu', 'i', 'b'))
    n = len(g)
    if not (n > 0 and len(m) == n and i >= 1 and b >= 1 and
            all(type(x) is int and x >= 0 for x in g + m) and
            g == sorted(g, reverse=True) and m == sorted(m, reverse=True) and
            sum(g) - sum(m) == i*b):
        raise ValueError('invalid signed-strip input')
    l = [g[j] + n - 1 - j for j in range(n)]
    a = [m[k] + n - 1 - k for k in range(n)]
    matrix = [[int(a[k] <= l[j] and (l[j] - a[k]) % i == 0)
               for k in range(n)] for j in range(n)]
    value = int(fmpz_mat(matrix).det())
    answers.append(value)
print(json.dumps(answers, separators=(',', ':')))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Specialized symbolic software can implement the source and target multiplicity comparisons for partitions in the stated degree and tail regimes. The source gives recurrences and arithmetic checks; the displayed program blocks contain no implementation text. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT — the source contains no game dynamics, physical simulation model, or simulation accuracy contract. |
| tools | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | A symbolic coefficient evaluator can use signed lookups and exact character recurrences to reproduce plethysm multiplicities within the specified character model. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper supplies exact recurrences, monotone upper certificates, and transported lower bounds that can reduce explicit multiplicity computations. |
| data_structures | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Partition prefix ranks and downward-closed arrays provide a concrete representation for exact multiplicity tables and lower-bound propagation. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT — the source describes independent coefficient destinations and degree barriers, but gives no accelerator kernel, device arithmetic contract, or transfer model. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT — the source establishes representation multiplicities, not a security assumption or cryptographic primitive. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT — the source gives exact representation-theoretic comparisons, with no learning objective, estimator, or trained model. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT — maxima propagate proved multiplicity lower bounds; the source supplies no optimisation objective, feasible decision model, or operations-research guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT — the source establishes degree barriers and read-only earlier tables within its coefficient computation, but supplies no distributed communication or fault model. |
| other (representation_theory_and_algebraic_combinatorics) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Representation theory and algebraic combinatorics are direct applications: the theorem compares composed symmetric powers over finite-dimensional complex spaces and yields Schur-positivity. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-field** (hypothesis): The theorem concerns finite-dimensional complex vector spaces and the sixth symmetric-power comparison with integer $b\ge6$. Blocked use: ['Extension to other fields, infinite-dimensional spaces, or other symmetric-power indices without a separate argument.']
- **L-canonical** (counterexample_scope): The multiplicity comparison does not imply injectivity of a prescribed canonical Foulkes–Howe map. Blocked use: ['Using the existence theorem as a certificate that the canonical map has trivial kernel.']
- **L-section** (nonconstructive): Complete reducibility supplies an equivariant section through an invariant complement; the section need not be a prescribed canonical map. Blocked use: ['Treating the theorem as an explicit algorithm producing the canonical injection.']
- **L-first-row** (hypothesis): The first-row certificate assumes that the partition tail sum is at most $b$. Blocked use: ['Applying the first-row certificate outside its tail condition.']
- **L-power** (hypothesis): The power certificate requires a positive known target multiplicity, integer $C$, and positive degree $d_0$. Blocked use: ['Amplifying an unproved seed or multiplying multiplicity dimensions without the highest-weight argument.']
- **L-truncation** (approximation): The Laurent truncation is justified by source support bounds; clipping is valid only when discarded terms are zero. Blocked use: ['Reusing the window at other tail cutoffs without proving new support bounds.']
- **L-execution** (formal_scope): Checking source identities and recorded numeric rows does not execute the mathematical programs. Blocked use: ['Counting an integrity check as an independent execution of all coefficient comparisons.']
- **L-runtime** (hypothesis): The certificate arithmetic uses exact integers and assertions; arithmetic bounds and range coverage are part of the argument. Blocked use: ['Replacing exact arithmetic with floating-point comparisons or disabling the required assertions.']
- **L-global** (formal_scope): Finite computations supply finite nonnegativity assertions; the universal theorem also uses the proved reductions and large-degree argument. Blocked use: ['Promoting a small finite subroutine check to verification of the universal theorem.']
- **L-artifacts** (unknown): Executable appendix bodies are ABSENT from the supplied repaired text; the reproduction claims cannot be independently established from those bodies alone. Blocked use: ['Claiming a full computational reproduction from the supplied repaired text alone.']

          "quote_sha256": "a18d32c8d1befb2f9989b3d416fd9291beaba7ae981d7c2d3ff6fdb817894a41"

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the sixth-symmetric-power case of Foulkes' conjecture. For every integer <i>b</i> ≥ 6 and every finite-dimensional complex vector space <i>V</i>, there is a $`\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}\nolimits (V)`$-equivariant injection $`\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}\nolimits ^6(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}\nolimits ^b V)\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}\nolimits ^b(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}\nolimits ^6 V)`$.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index
